- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing test**

Write the fixture through a node script, because the Write tool decodes backslash-u sequences. Save this with the Write tool as `build-cross-language.mjs` in your session scratchpad, not in the project.

```js
// Writes internal/storymaker/testdata/narration/cross_language.json. Run from the worktree root.
// Invisible and astral characters use fromCodePoint. Every number below is counted by hand from the rules.
import fs from 'node:fs';

const c = String.fromCodePoint;
const NBSP = c(0x00A0), IDEO = c(0x3000), LS = c(0x2028), BOM = c(0xFEFF);
const GREEK_Q = c(0x037E), KICHI = c(0x20BB7), GRIN = c(0x1F600);

const HAN_A = '今日は天気がいいです。', HAN_B = '明日は雨が降るでしょう。';
const HI_A = 'आज मौसम बहुत अच्छा है।', HI_B = 'कल बारिश होगी और हवा भी चलेगी।';
const EL_A = 'Η μέρα είναι όμορφη σήμερα' + GREEK_Q, EL_B = 'Ναι, πολύ όμορφη σήμερα.';

const rows = [
    // U+00A0 collapses to a space. "Anna kommt " is 11 units when the marker comes. 11 + "heute spät." (11) = 22. 4 words.
    { input: 'Anna' + NBSP + 'kommt {1}heute  spät.', plain: 'Anna kommt heute spät.', cues: { 1: 11 }, chars_total: 22, width: 22, words: 4 },
    // U+3000 at both ends and around a marker. "Das ist gut. " is 13 units. 13 + "Ja und nein, sagt sie." (22) = 35. 8 words.
    { input: IDEO + 'Das ist gut.' + IDEO + IDEO + '{2}' + IDEO + 'Ja und nein, sagt sie.' + IDEO, plain: 'Das ist gut. Ja und nein, sagt sie.', cues: { 2: 13 }, chars_total: 35, width: 35, words: 8 },
    // Han and kana: 11 + 12 = 23 units, all wide so width 46, one CJK run of 23 so ceil(23/2) = 12 words.
    { input: HAN_A + '{1}' + HAN_B, plain: HAN_A + HAN_B, cues: { 1: 11 }, chars_total: 23, width: 46, words: 12 },
    // Hindi: 22 + space + 30 = 53 units, none wide. 5 + 7 = 12 words. Marks count as code points.
    { input: HI_A + ' ' + HI_B, plain: HI_A + ' ' + HI_B, cues: {}, chars_total: 53, width: 53, words: 12 },
    // Greek: 27 + space + 24 = 52 units, none wide. 5 + 4 = 9 words. U+037E is one unit.
    { input: EL_A + ' ' + EL_B, plain: EL_A + ' ' + EL_B, cues: {}, chars_total: 52, width: 52, words: 9 },
    // A German quote: U+201E opens, U+201C closes. 47 + space = 48 units when the marker comes. 48 + "Dann ging er langsam nach Hause." (32) = 80. None wide. 9 + 6 = 15 words.
    { input: 'Er sagte ganz laut und deutlich: „Das ist gut.“ {1}Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.', plain: 'Er sagte ganz laut und deutlich: „Das ist gut.“ Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.', cues: { 1: 48 }, chars_total: 80, width: 80, words: 15 },
    // Surrogate pairs: U+20BB7 (2 units, wide, CJK), 野, 家, U+1F600 (2 units, not wide, not CJK), space, o, k.
    // Units 2+1+1+2+1+1+1 = 9. Width 2+2+2+1+1+1+1 = 10. Words: CJK run of 3 gives 2, the emoji run 1, "ok" 1, so 4.
    { input: KICHI + '{1}野家' + GRIN + ' ok', plain: KICHI + '野家' + GRIN + ' ok', cues: { 1: 2 }, chars_total: 9, width: 10, words: 4 },
    // U+2028 and U+FEFF collapse as one run. The cue stood at 11, past the trimmed end, so it clamps to 10.
    { input: 'Hallo' + LS + BOM + 'Welt {3}', plain: 'Hallo Welt', cues: { 3: 10 }, chars_total: 10, width: 10, words: 2 },
    // Only spaces and a marker: nothing to speak.
    { input: '  {1}  ', plain: '', cues: { 1: 0 }, chars_total: 0, width: 0, words: 0 }
];
for (const row of rows) {
    if (row.plain.length !== row.chars_total) throw new Error('the hand count is wrong for: ' + row.plain);
}
fs.mkdirSync('internal/storymaker/testdata/narration', { recursive: true });
fs.writeFileSync('internal/storymaker/testdata/narration/cross_language.json', JSON.stringify(rows, null, 2) + '\n');
```

Create `internal/storymaker/narration_test.go`:

```go
package storymaker

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"os"
	"strconv"
	"testing"
)

// crossRow is one row of testdata/narration/cross_language.json, which scripts/storymaker-tests/16-narration.mjs reads too.
type crossRow struct {
	Input      string         `json:"input"`
	Plain      string         `json:"plain"`
	Cues       map[string]int `json:"cues"`
	CharsTotal int            `json:"chars_total"`
	Width      int            `json:"width"`
	Words      int            `json:"words"`
}

func crossRows(t *testing.T) []crossRow {
	t.Helper()
	data, err := os.ReadFile("testdata/narration/cross_language.json")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var rows []crossRow
	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &rows); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if len(rows) == 0 {
		t.Fatal("the fixture has no rows")
	}
	return rows
}

func TestParseMarkersMatchesCrossLanguageFixture(t *testing.T) {
	for _, row := range crossRows(t) {
		plain, cues := parseMarkers(row.Input)
		if plain != row.Plain {
			t.Errorf("%q: plain %q, want %q", row.Input, plain, row.Plain)
		}
		if got := StripMarkers(row.Input); got != row.Plain {
			t.Errorf("%q: StripMarkers %q, want %q", row.Input, got, row.Plain)
		}
		if len(cues) != len(row.Cues) {
			t.Errorf("%q: cues %v, want %v", row.Input, cues, row.Cues)
		}
		for n, at := range cues {
			if want, ok := row.Cues[strconv.Itoa(n)]; !ok || want != at {
				t.Errorf("%q: cue %d at %d, want %d", row.Input, n, at, want)
			}
		}
		if got := UTF16Len(plain); got != row.CharsTotal {
			t.Errorf("%q: UTF16Len %d, want %d", row.Plain, got, row.CharsTotal)
		}
		if got := TextWidth(plain); got != row.Width {
			t.Errorf("%q: TextWidth %d, want %d", row.Plain, got, row.Width)
		}
		if got := WordCount(plain); got != row.Words {
			t.Errorf("%q: WordCount %d, want %d", row.Plain, got, row.Words)
		}
	}
}

func TestBlocks(t *testing.T) {
	annaVoice := Voice{Provider: "Phonon", Voice: "nele", Language: "de"}
	story := &Story{
		Voice: &Voice{Provider: " ElevenLabs ", Voice: "v1", Language: "de"},
		Cast: []CastMember{
			{ID: "anna", Character: "char.a", Voice: &annaVoice},
			{ID: "ben", Character: "char.b"},
		},
		Scenes: []Scene{
			{ID: "s1", Narration: "Hallo {1}Welt.", Beats: []Beat{
				{Do: "say", Who: "anna", Text: "Hi {2}du."},
				{Do: "wait", Seconds: 1},
				{Do: "say", Who: "ben", Text: "   "},
				{Do: "say", Who: "ben", Text: "{3}"},
				{Do: "say", Who: "anna"},
				// ben is not in the scene's actors, so he is off stage. His audio still plays.
				{Do: "say", Who: "ben", Text: "Ich bin nicht zu sehen."},
			}},
			{ID: "s2", Silent: true, Narration: "Wird nicht gesprochen.", Beats: []Beat{{Do: "say", Who: "anna", Text: "Trotzdem."}}},
			{ID: "s3", Kind: "code", Speaker: "anna", Narration: "Der Code spricht.", Beats: []Beat{{Do: "say", Who: "anna", Text: "Nie."}}},
			{ID: "s4", Narration: "  {1}  "},
		},
	}
	storyVoice := Voice{Provider: "elevenlabs", Voice: "v1", Language: "de"}
	cast := Voice{Provider: "phonon", Voice: "nele", Language: "de"}
	want := []NarrationBlock{
		{ID: "s1:n", Kind: BlockKindNarration, SceneID: "s1", SceneIndex: 0, Beat: -1, Speaker: "narrator", Voice: storyVoice, Text: "Hallo Welt."},
		{ID: "s1:b0", Kind: BlockKindDialogue, SceneID: "s1", SceneIndex: 0, Beat: 0, Speaker: "anna", Voice: cast, Text: "Hi du."},
		{ID: "s1:b5", Kind: BlockKindDialogue, SceneID: "s1", SceneIndex: 0, Beat: 5, Speaker: "ben", Voice: storyVoice, Text: "Ich bin nicht zu sehen."},
		{ID: "s2:b0", Kind: BlockKindDialogue, SceneID: "s2", SceneIndex: 1, Beat: 0, Speaker: "anna", Voice: cast, Text: "Trotzdem."},
		{ID: "s3:n", Kind: BlockKindNarration, SceneID: "s3", SceneIndex: 2, Beat: -1, Speaker: "anna", Voice: cast, Text: "Der Code spricht."},
	}
	got := Blocks(story)
	if len(got) != len(want) {
		t.Fatalf("%d blocks, want %d: %+v", len(got), len(want), got)
	}
	for i := range want {
		if got[i] != want[i] {
			t.Errorf("block %d:\n got %+v\nwant %+v", i, got[i], want[i])
		}
	}
}

func TestBlocksWithoutAVoice(t *testing.T) {
	got := Blocks(&Story{Scenes: []Scene{{ID: "s1", Narration: "Hallo."}}})
	if len(got) != 1 || got[0].Voice.Provider != "" || got[0].Speaker != "narrator" {
		t.Fatalf("got %+v", got)
	}
}
```

```js
// Task N1: the shared narration fixture. Go reads the same file in internal/storymaker/narration_test.go.
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { plain, readJSON } from '../storymaker-test-support.mjs';
import { t } from '../storymaker-test-context.mjs';

const { check } = t;
const SM = t.core();
const rows = readJSON('internal/storymaker/testdata/narration/cross_language.json');

check('narration: parse matches the shared fixture', () => {
    assert.ok(rows.length > 0, 'the fixture has rows');
    for (const row of rows) {
        const got = SM.narration.parse(row.input);
        assert.equal(got.plain, row.plain, 'plain of ' + row.input);
        assert.deepEqual(plain(got.cues), row.cues, 'cues of ' + row.input);
        assert.equal(got.length, row.chars_total, 'length of ' + row.input);
        assert.equal(got.words, row.words, 'words of ' + row.input);
        assert.equal(SM.ranges.width(got.plain), row.width, 'width of ' + row.input);
    }
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement**

After G3, `internal/storymaker/narration.go` holds only `IsJSSpace`. Overwrite the whole file with this text.

```go
package storymaker

import (
	"regexp"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

// Block kinds and piece joins of the narration model.
const (
	BlockKindNarration = "narration"
	BlockKindDialogue  = "dialogue"
	JoinSentence       = "sentence" // 350 ms pause after this piece
	JoinSplit          = "split"    // 150 ms pause after this piece
)

// NarrationBlock is one spoken unit of a story: the narration of a scene, or the text of one say beat.
type NarrationBlock struct {
	ID         string // "<scene>:n" or "<scene>:b<beatIndex>"
	Kind       string
	SceneID    string
	SceneIndex int
	Beat       int    // beat index; -1 for kind narration
	Speaker    string // "narrator" or a cast id
	Voice      Voice  // resolved; Provider == "" when nothing resolves; Provider is in NormalizeProvider form
	Text       string // StripMarkers of the source text
}

// narrationMarker is the marker regex of narration.js. cuePattern in validate.go is narrower, so it cannot be reused.
var narrationMarker = regexp.MustCompile(`\{(\d+)\}`)

// Blocks lists the spoken blocks of a story in scene order, inside a scene the narration first and the say blocks in
// beat order. silent silences only the scene narration. A say whose actor is off stage still makes a block.
func Blocks(s *Story) []NarrationBlock {
	if s == nil {
		return nil
	}
	var out []NarrationBlock
	for i := range s.Scenes {
		sc := &s.Scenes[i]
		if !sc.Silent {
			if text := StripMarkers(sc.Narration); text != "" {
				speaker := sc.Speaker
				if speaker == "" {
					speaker = "narrator"
				}
				out = append(out, NarrationBlock{
					ID: sc.ID + ":n", Kind: BlockKindNarration, SceneID: sc.ID, SceneIndex: i, Beat: -1,
					Speaker: speaker, Voice: blockVoice(s, speaker), Text: text,
				})
			}
		}
		if sc.Kind == "code" {
			continue
		}
		for j := range sc.Beats {
			b := &sc.Beats[j]
			if b.Do != "say" {
				continue
			}
			text := StripMarkers(b.Text)
			if text == "" {
				continue
			}
			out = append(out, NarrationBlock{
				ID: sc.ID + ":b" + strconv.Itoa(j), Kind: BlockKindDialogue, SceneID: sc.ID, SceneIndex: i, Beat: j,
				Speaker: b.Who, Voice: blockVoice(s, b.Who), Text: text,
			})
		}
	}
	return out
}

// blockVoice is the cast member's voice when the speaker is a cast id with a voice, else the story voice.
func blockVoice(s *Story, speaker string) Voice {
	for i := range s.Cast {
		if s.Cast[i].ID == speaker && s.Cast[i].Voice != nil {
			return normalizedVoice(*s.Cast[i].Voice)
		}
	}
	if s.Voice != nil {
		return normalizedVoice(*s.Voice)
	}
	return Voice{}
}

func normalizedVoice(v Voice) Voice {
	v.Provider = NormalizeProvider(v.Provider)
	return v
}

// NormalizeProvider is the trimmed, lower-cased provider id. The validator accepts mixed case without rewriting it.
func NormalizeProvider(id string) string {
	return strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace(id))
}

// StripMarkers is byte for byte SM.narration.parse(text).plain of ui/js/storymaker/narration.js.
func StripMarkers(text string) string {
	plain, _ := parseMarkers(text)
	return plain
}

// parseMarkers follows narration.js parse: the plain text and the first UTF-16 offset of each marker, clamped to the
// trimmed length.
func parseMarkers(text string) (string, map[int]int) {
	var b strings.Builder
	cues := map[int]int{}
	appendChunk := func(chunk string) {
		chunk = collapseJSSpace(chunk)
		if b.Len() == 0 || strings.HasSuffix(b.String(), " ") {
			chunk = strings.TrimPrefix(chunk, " ")
		}
		b.WriteString(chunk)
	}
	last := 0
	for _, m := range narrationMarker.FindAllStringSubmatchIndex(text, -1) {
		appendChunk(text[last:m[0]])
		if n, err := strconv.Atoi(text[m[2]:m[3]]); err == nil {
			if _, seen := cues[n]; !seen {
				cues[n] = UTF16Len(b.String())
			}
		}
		last = m[1]
	}
	appendChunk(text[last:])
	plain := strings.TrimSuffix(b.String(), " ")
	length := UTF16Len(plain)
	for n, at := range cues {
		if at > length {
			cues[n] = length
		}
	}
	return plain, cues
}

// collapseJSSpace turns each run of JS whitespace into one U+0020, like replace(/\s+/g, ' ').
func collapseJSSpace(s string) string {
	var b strings.Builder
	inSpace := false
	for _, r := range s {
		if IsJSSpace(r) {
			if !inSpace {
				b.WriteByte(' ')
			}
			inSpace = true
			continue
		}
		inSpace = false
		b.WriteRune(r)
	}
	return b.String()
}

// IsJSSpace is exactly the JS regex class \s. Go's \s is ASCII only.
func IsJSSpace(r rune) bool {
	switch {
	case r >= 0x0009 && r <= 0x000D, r == 0x0020, r == 0x00A0, r == 0x1680,
		r >= 0x2000 && r <= 0x200A, r == 0x2028, r == 0x2029, r == 0x202F,
		r == 0x205F, r == 0x3000, r == 0xFEFF:
		return true
	}
	return false
}

// UTF16Len counts the UTF-16 units of s, the unit JS string offsets use.
func UTF16Len(s string) int {
	n := 0
	for _, r := range s {
		n++
		if r >= 0x10000 {
			n++
		}
	}
	return n
}

// TextWidth counts East Asian wide characters as 2 and everything else as 1. It equals ranges.width in JS.
func TextWidth(s string) int {
	n := 0
	for _, r := range s {
		n++
		if inUCDTable(ucdEastAsianWide, r) {
			n++
		}
	}
	return n
}

// WordCount skips JS whitespace. A run of n CJK word characters counts ceil(n/2), a run of anything else counts 1.
// It equals ranges.wordCount in JS.
func WordCount(s string) int {
	words, cjk := 0, 0
	inOther := false
	for _, r := range s {
		switch {
		case IsJSSpace(r):
			words += (cjk + 1) / 2
			cjk, inOther = 0, false
		case inUCDTable(ucdCJKWord, r):
			inOther = false
			cjk++
		default:
			words += (cjk + 1) / 2
			cjk = 0
			if !inOther {
				words++
				inOther = true
			}
		}
	}
	return words + (cjk+1)/2
}
```

Edit `internal/storymaker/validate.go`. The old text below is as G4 left it. `StripMarkers` applies the same rule as the old body: `narrationMarker` matches the same markers as `sayMarkerPattern`, because Go's `\d` is ASCII only, and a text of only spaces or only a marker gives `""`. `sayMarkerPattern` has no other user, so it goes too. `regexp` and `strings` stay imported, because `cuePattern` and the rest of the file use them.

Old text:

```go
// sayMarkerPattern is the marker form of the player's narration parser: any count of ASCII digits, so wider than cuePattern.
var sayMarkerPattern = regexp.MustCompile(`\{[0-9]+\}`)

// sayHasText is true when a say's text keeps a character after its markers and JS whitespace go. It is the same test as Blocks
// (StripMarkers(text) is not empty) and the player (parsed.words > 0), so a say of only spaces counts as a say without text.
func sayHasText(text string) bool {
	return strings.TrimFunc(sayMarkerPattern.ReplaceAllString(text, ""), IsJSSpace) != ""
}
```

New text:

```go
// sayHasText is true when a say's text keeps a character after its markers and JS whitespace go. It is the same test as Blocks
// (StripMarkers(text) is not empty) and the player (parsed.words > 0), so a say of only spaces counts as a say without text.
func sayHasText(text string) bool {
	return StripMarkers(text) != ""
}
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Add the `pieces` field to the fixture through a node script. Save this with the Write tool as `add-cross-language-pieces.mjs` in your session scratchpad.

```js
// Adds the expected pieces to cross_language.json. Run from the worktree root. Pieces are for limit 400, the default of ChunkLimit.
import fs from 'node:fs';

const file = 'internal/storymaker/testdata/narration/cross_language.json';
const c = String.fromCodePoint;
const GREEK_Q = c(0x037E), KICHI = c(0x20BB7), GRIN = c(0x1F600);
const HI_A = 'आज मौसम बहुत अच्छा है।', HI_B = 'कल बारिश होगी और हवा भी चलेगी।';
const EL_A = 'Η μέρα είναι όμορφη σήμερα' + GREEK_Q, EL_B = 'Ναι, πολύ όμορφη σήμερα.';

const pieces = [
    // One sentence, width 22 and 4 words.
    [{ text: 'Anna kommt heute spät.', chars: 22, join: '' }],
    // "Das ist gut." has width 12, so it merges into the next sentence. The merged part keeps the last member's join.
    [{ text: 'Das ist gut. Ja und nein, sagt sie.', chars: 35, join: '' }],
    // Width 22 and 24, words ceil(11/2) = 6 and ceil(12/2) = 6. Neither is a fragment.
    [{ text: '今日は天気がいいです。', chars: 11, join: 'sentence' }, { text: '明日は雨が降るでしょう。', chars: 12, join: '' }],
    // The danda ends a sentence. The space after it belongs to the first piece: 22 + 1 = 23.
    [{ text: HI_A, chars: 23, join: 'sentence' }, { text: HI_B, chars: 30, join: '' }],
    // U+037E ends a sentence. 27 + 1 = 28, then 24.
    [{ text: EL_A, chars: 28, join: 'sentence' }, { text: EL_B, chars: 24, join: '' }],
    // U+201C closes the German quote, so the dot before it ends the sentence and the quote stays on it. 47 + 1 = 48, then 32.
    [{ text: 'Er sagte ganz laut und deutlich: „Das ist gut.“', chars: 48, join: 'sentence' }, { text: 'Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.', chars: 32, join: '' }],
    // A fragment with no neighbour stays as it is.
    [{ text: KICHI + '野家' + GRIN + ' ok', chars: 9, join: '' }],
    [{ text: 'Hallo Welt', chars: 10, join: '' }],
    []
];
const rows = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8'));
if (rows.length !== pieces.length) throw new Error('the fixture has ' + rows.length + ' rows');
rows.forEach((row, i) => {
    const sum = pieces[i].reduce((total, p) => total + p.chars, 0);
    if (sum !== row.chars_total) throw new Error('the chars of row ' + i + ' sum to ' + sum);
    row.pieces = pieces[i];
});
fs.writeFileSync(file, JSON.stringify(rows, null, 2) + '\n');
```

```powershell
node <session scratchpad>/add-cross-language-pieces.mjs
```

```go
func TestSplitPiecesMatchesCrossLanguageFixture(t *testing.T) {
	for _, row := range crossRows(t) {
		got := SplitPieces(row.Plain, ChunkLimit(""))
		if len(got) != len(row.Pieces) {
			t.Errorf("%q: %d pieces, want %d: %+v", row.Plain, len(got), len(row.Pieces), got)
			continue
		}
		sum := 0
		for i, p := range got {
			if p != row.Pieces[i] {
				t.Errorf("%q piece %d: got %+v, want %+v", row.Plain, i, p, row.Pieces[i])
			}
			sum += p.Chars
		}
		if sum != UTF16Len(row.Plain) {
			t.Errorf("%q: chars sum to %d, want %d", row.Plain, sum, UTF16Len(row.Plain))
		}
	}
}

func TestSplitPiecesSentencesAndMerges(t *testing.T) {
	// long has 37 runes and 7 words, other has 39 runes and 7 words, so neither is a fragment.
	const long = "Das ist ein etwas längerer Satz hier."
	const other = "Und noch ein weiterer langer Satz hier."
	cases := []struct {
		name  string
		text  string
		limit int
		want  []Piece
	}{
		// "Genau." has width 6. 6 + space + 37 = 44.
		{"a first fragment merges into the next", "Genau. " + long, 400, []Piece{{"Genau. " + long, 44, ""}}},
		{"a last fragment merges into the previous", long + " Genau.", 400, []Piece{{long + " Genau.", 44, ""}}},
		// 37 + space = 38. "Ja." + space + 39 = 43.
		{"a middle fragment merges into the next", long + " Ja. " + other, 400, []Piece{{long, 38, JoinSentence}, {"Ja. " + other, 43, ""}}},
		// The merged span of 44 runes is over the limit of 40, so "Genau." stays. 6 + space = 7.
		{"no merge over the limit", "Genau. " + long, 40, []Piece{{"Genau.", 7, JoinSentence}, {long, 37, ""}}},
		// The first sentence has 36 runes, with its closing quote. 36 + space = 37. The second has 32 runes.
		{"closers stay on the sentence", `Er sagt es ganz laut: "Das ist gut!" Dann geht er langsam nach Hause.`, 400, []Piece{
			{`Er sagt es ganz laut: "Das ist gut!"`, 37, JoinSentence}, {"Dann geht er langsam nach Hause.", 32, ""}}},
		// The dot of 3.14 is followed by a digit. The first sentence has 34 runes, 34 + space = 35. The second has 24.
		{"a decimal point is no sentence end", "Version 3.14 ist da und läuft gut. Das freut uns alle sehr.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Version 3.14 ist da und läuft gut.", 35, JoinSentence}, {"Das freut uns alle sehr.", 24, ""}}},
		// The next five stay one piece. Cut at the inner dot, both sides would be long enough not to merge back.
		// 34 + space + 45 = 80.
		{"an ordinal is no sentence end", "Die Firewall wurde am Ende des 19. Jahrhunderts in einem kleinen Labor erfunden.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Die Firewall wurde am Ende des 19. Jahrhunderts in einem kleinen Labor erfunden.", 80, ""}}},
		// 28 + space + 35 = 64. "z" and "B" are single letters.
		{"a single letter is no sentence end", "Viele Geräte im Netzwerk, z. B. Router und Switches, nutzen das.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Viele Geräte im Netzwerk, z. B. Router und Switches, nutzen das.", 64, ""}}},
		// 24 + space + 35 = 60.
		{"a title is no sentence end", "Im kleinen Labor von Dr. Müller entstand die erste Firewall.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Im kleinen Labor von Dr. Müller entstand die erste Firewall.", 60, ""}}},
		// 40 + space + 31 = 72.
		{"an abbreviation is no sentence end", "Das kleine Gerät liegt im Handel bei ca. 20 Euro und damit sehr günstig.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Das kleine Gerät liegt im Handel bei ca. 20 Euro und damit sehr günstig.", 72, ""}}},
		// 39 + space + 29 = 69. The first dot of "e.g." is followed by a letter, the second by a lower-case word.
		{"a dot before a lower-case word is no sentence end", "Many small devices on the network, e.g. routers and switches, use it.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Many small devices on the network, e.g. routers and switches, use it.", 69, ""}}},
		// A year has four digits and ends the sentence. 25 + space = 26. The second has 31 runes.
		{"a year ends the sentence", "Das geschah im Jahr 1990. Danach wurde alles ganz anders.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Das geschah im Jahr 1990.", 26, JoinSentence}, {"Danach wurde alles ganz anders.", 31, ""}}},
		// A closer follows the dot, so the dot still ends the sentence. 38 + space = 39. The second has 31 runes.
		{"a dot before a closer ends the sentence", `Das Kapitel heißt einfach "Nummer 19." Danach wurde alles ganz anders.`, 400, []Piece{
			{`Das Kapitel heißt einfach "Nummer 19."`, 39, JoinSentence}, {"Danach wurde alles ganz anders.", 31, ""}}},
		// U+201C closes a German quote, so the dot before it ends the sentence and the quote stays on it. 47 + space = 48.
		// The second has 32 runes.
		{"a German closing quote ends the sentence", "Er sagte ganz laut und deutlich: „Das ist gut.“ Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Er sagte ganz laut und deutlich: „Das ist gut.“", 48, JoinSentence}, {"Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.", 32, ""}}},
		// U+2018 closes the single form. Again 47 + space = 48.
		{"a German single closing quote ends the sentence", "Er sagte ganz laut und deutlich: ‚Das ist gut!‘ Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Er sagte ganz laut und deutlich: ‚Das ist gut!‘", 48, JoinSentence}, {"Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.", 32, ""}}},
		// U+00AB closes a German chevron quote. It stays on the sentence the ellipsis ended, so the next piece has no stray
		// quote. 53 + space = 54.
		{"a hard terminator keeps a German closer", "Sie rief ihm ganz laut und deutlich zu: »Warte doch…« Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.", 400, []Piece{
			{"Sie rief ihm ganz laut und deutlich zu: »Warte doch…«", 54, JoinSentence}, {"Dann ging er langsam nach Hause.", 32, ""}}},
		// U+201C opens a Chinese quote and no space follows the stop, so the quote starts the next sentence. 13 runes each.
		{"a quote that opens after a hard terminator goes to the next sentence", "他说今天的天气真的非常好。“明天的天气也会这样好。”", 400, []Piece{
			{"他说今天的天气真的非常好。", 13, JoinSentence}, {"“明天的天气也会这样好。”", 13, ""}}},
		// The vowel sign belongs to the word, so its last consonant is no single letter. 22 + space = 23. The second has 24.
		{"a consonant after a vowel sign is no single letter", "आज हमने बहुत सारा काम. कल फिर से बहुत सारा काम.", 400, []Piece{
			{"आज हमने बहुत सारा काम.", 23, JoinSentence}, {"कल फिर से बहुत सारा काम.", 24, ""}}},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		got := SplitPieces(c.text, c.limit)
		if len(got) != len(c.want) {
			t.Errorf("%s: got %+v, want %+v", c.name, got, c.want)
			continue
		}
		for i := range got {
			if got[i] != c.want[i] {
				t.Errorf("%s piece %d: got %+v, want %+v", c.name, i, got[i], c.want[i])
			}
		}
	}
}

func TestSplitPiecesKeepsHanSentencesApart(t *testing.T) {
	// 11 characters: width 22, words ceil(11/2) = 6, so it is no fragment and nothing merges up to the limit.
	got := SplitPieces(strings.Repeat("今日は天気がいいです。", 5), 400)
	if len(got) != 5 {
		t.Fatalf("%d pieces, want 5: %+v", len(got), got)
	}
}

func TestSplitPiecesLongSentences(t *testing.T) {
	// words(n) has 5n-1 runes.
	words := func(n int) string { return strings.TrimSpace(strings.Repeat("wort ", n)) }

	// Runes: 49, ", ", 44, ", ", 54, "." = 152, so the middle is 76. The commas stand at 49 (distance 27) and 95 (distance 19).
	// The left part keeps its comma: 96 runes, 97 units with the space. The right part has 55.
	text := words(10) + ", " + words(9) + ", " + words(11) + "."
	assertPieces(t, "comma", SplitPieces(text, 100), []Piece{
		{words(10) + ", " + words(9) + ",", 97, JoinSplit},
		{words(11) + ".", 55, ""},
	})

	// 120 words are 599 runes with no comma. "Google" has limit 200. The spaces stand at 5k-1, so the cuts fall at 199 and 399.
	// Three parts of 199 runes (40 words each). The first two also own a space.
	block := NarrationBlock{Voice: Voice{Provider: "Google"}, Text: words(120)}
	assertPieces(t, "whitespace", PiecesOf(block), []Piece{
		{words(40), 200, JoinSplit},
		{words(40), 200, JoinSplit},
		{words(40), 199, ""},
	})

	// 250 astral characters with no comma and no space: cuts at code points, 2 UTF-16 units each.
	kichi := string(rune(0x20BB7))
	assertPieces(t, "code point", SplitPieces(strings.Repeat(kichi, 250), 100), []Piece{
		{strings.Repeat(kichi, 100), 200, JoinSplit},
		{strings.Repeat(kichi, 100), 200, JoinSplit},
		{strings.Repeat(kichi, 50), 100, ""},
	})
}

func assertPieces(t *testing.T, name string, got, want []Piece) {
	t.Helper()
	if len(got) != len(want) {
		t.Fatalf("%s: %d pieces, want %d: %+v", name, len(got), len(want), got)
	}
	for i := range got {
		if got[i] != want[i] {
			t.Errorf("%s piece %d: got %+v, want %+v", name, i, got[i], want[i])
		}
		if runeLen(got[i].Text) > 200 {
			t.Errorf("%s piece %d has %d runes", name, i, runeLen(got[i].Text))
		}
	}
}

func TestSplitPiecesEmptyAndChunkLimit(t *testing.T) {
	if got := SplitPieces("", 400); got != nil {
		t.Errorf("empty text gave %+v", got)
	}
	if got := SplitPieces("   ", 400); got != nil {
		t.Errorf("whitespace gave %+v", got)
	}
	for provider, want := range map[string]int{"Phonon ": 2000, "SANOTTS": 2000, "Google": 200, "elevenlabs": 400, "": 400} {
		if got := ChunkLimit(provider); got != want {
			t.Errorf("ChunkLimit(%q) = %d, want %d", provider, got, want)
		}
	}
}

func TestPieceKey(t *testing.T) {
	sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte("google|Kore|de-de|Hallo Welt."))
	want := hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
	if got := PieceKey(Voice{Provider: "google", Voice: "Kore", Language: "de-de"}, "Hallo Welt."); got != want {
		t.Errorf("key %s, want %s", got, want)
	}
	if got := PieceKey(Voice{Provider: " Google ", Voice: "Kore", Language: "DE-de "}, "Hallo Welt."); got != want {
		t.Errorf("provider and language case changed the key: %s", got)
	}
	if PieceKey(Voice{Provider: "google", Voice: "kore", Language: "de-de"}, "Hallo Welt.") == want {
		t.Error("the voice must stay as written")
	}
	if PieceKey(Voice{Provider: "google", Voice: "Kore", Language: "de-de"}, "hallo welt.") == want {
		t.Error("the piece text must stay as written")
	}
}
```

New text:

```go
// Piece is one provider call of a block.
type Piece struct {
	Text  string // what is synthesised and hashed; no leading or trailing whitespace
	Chars int    // UTF-16 units of the source span including the whitespace after it
	Join  string // how it joins the next piece; "" on the last
}

const (
	fragmentWidth = 20 // a part below this width merges
	fragmentWords = 3  // a part with this many words or fewer merges
	minSplitRunes = 40 // each side of a comma split has at least this many runes
)

// pieceSpan is one part of a text, in runes. [start, end) is the source span with its whitespace, [lo, hi) the text.
type pieceSpan struct {
	start, lo, hi, end int
	join               string
}

// SplitPieces cuts text into one piece per sentence, splits a sentence over limit runes, and merges fragments.
// Chars sums to UTF16Len(text). Empty text gives nil.
func SplitPieces(text string, limit int) []Piece {
	if limit < 1 {
		limit = 1
	}
	rs := []rune(text)
	var parts []pieceSpan
	for _, sentence := range sentenceSpans(rs) {
		split := splitLongSentence(rs, sentence, limit)
		for i := range split {
			split[i].join = JoinSplit
		}
		split[len(split)-1].join = JoinSentence
		parts = append(parts, split...)
	}
	if len(parts) == 0 {
		return nil
	}
	parts = mergeFragments(rs, parts, limit)
	parts[len(parts)-1].join = ""
	out := make([]Piece, 0, len(parts))
	for _, p := range parts {
		out = append(out, Piece{Text: string(rs[p.lo:p.hi]), Chars: UTF16Len(string(rs[p.start:p.end])), Join: p.join})
	}
	return out
}

// PiecesOf splits a block with the chunk limit of its provider.
func PiecesOf(b NarrationBlock) []Piece {
	return SplitPieces(b.Text, ChunkLimit(b.Voice.Provider))
}

// ChunkLimit is the longest text of one provider call, in runes.
func ChunkLimit(provider string) int {
	switch NormalizeProvider(provider) {
	case "phonon", "sanotts":
		return 2000
	case "google":
		return 200
	}
	return 400
}

// PieceKey is the lower-case hex sha256 of provider|voice|lang|pieceText. Provider and language ignore case, because the
// validator accepts mixed case without rewriting it. Voice and text stay as written.
func PieceKey(v Voice, pieceText string) string {
	provider := NormalizeProvider(v.Provider)
	lang := strings.ToLower(strings.TrimSpace(v.Language))
	sum := sha256.Sum256([]byte(provider + "|" + v.Voice + "|" + lang + "|" + pieceText))
	return hex.EncodeToString(sum[:])
}

// spanOf trims JS whitespace from both ends of [start, end) to find the text range.
func spanOf(rs []rune, start, end int) pieceSpan {
	lo, hi := start, end
	for lo < hi && IsJSSpace(rs[lo]) {
		lo++
	}
	for hi > lo && IsJSSpace(rs[hi-1]) {
		hi--
	}
	return pieceSpan{start: start, lo: lo, hi: hi, end: end}
}

// sentenceSpans cuts the text into contiguous sentences. The whitespace after a sentence belongs to its span.
func sentenceSpans(rs []rune) []pieceSpan {
	var out []pieceSpan
	start := 0
	for i := 0; i < len(rs); {
		stop := sentenceEnd(rs, i)
		if stop < 0 {
			i++
			continue
		}
		for stop < len(rs) && IsJSSpace(rs[stop]) {
			stop++
		}
		out = append(out, spanOf(rs, start, stop))
		start = stop
		i = stop
	}
	if tail := spanOf(rs, start, len(rs)); tail.lo < tail.hi {
		out = append(out, tail)
	}
	return out
}

// sentenceEnd returns the rune index just after the sentence that ends at i, closers included, or -1.
// A dot directly followed by whitespace can stay inside the sentence, see dotContinues.
func sentenceEnd(rs []rune, i int) int {
	r := rs[i]
	switch {
	case isASCIITerminator(r):
		j := i + 1
		for j < len(rs) && isSentenceCloser(rs[j]) {
			j++
		}
		if j < len(rs) && !IsJSSpace(rs[j]) {
			return -1
		}
		if r == '.' && j == i+1 && j < len(rs) && dotContinues(rs, i) {
			return -1
		}
		return j
	case isHardTerminator(r):
		j := i + 1
		for j < len(rs) && (isHardTerminator(rs[j]) || isASCIITerminator(rs[j]) || isSentenceCloser(rs[j])) && !opensNextSentence(rs, j) {
			j++
		}
		return j
	}
	return -1
}

func isASCIITerminator(r rune) bool { return r == '.' || r == '!' || r == '?' }

func isASCIIDigit(r rune) bool { return r >= '0' && r <= '9' }

// dotContinues says that the dot at i, directly followed by whitespace, stays inside its sentence. It does when the next
// word starts in lower case or with a digit, or when the token before the dot is 1 to 3 digits (an ordinal like "19."), a
// single letter ("z. B.", "e.g.") or a common abbreviation. A year of 4 digits still ends the sentence. A missed sentence
// end costs one longer piece, which still splits at the limit. A false one puts a pause and a new provider call inside
// the sentence. So the rule leans towards not splitting. A combining mark belongs to the token, because the last
// consonant of an Indic word after a vowel sign is no single letter.
func dotContinues(rs []rune, i int) bool {
	next := i + 1
	for next < len(rs) && IsJSSpace(rs[next]) {
		next++
	}
	if next < len(rs) && (unicode.IsLower(rs[next]) || isASCIIDigit(rs[next])) {
		return true
	}
	start := i
	for start > 0 && (unicode.IsLetter(rs[start-1]) || unicode.IsDigit(rs[start-1]) || unicode.IsMark(rs[start-1])) {
		start--
	}
	token := rs[start:i]
	if len(token) == 1 && unicode.IsLetter(token[0]) {
		return true
	}
	digits := 0
	for digits < len(token) && isASCIIDigit(token[digits]) {
		digits++
	}
	if digits == len(token) && digits >= 1 && digits <= 3 {
		return true
	}
	switch strings.ToLower(string(token)) {
	case "dr", "prof", "nr", "bzw", "ca", "usw", "etc", "vs", "mr", "mrs", "st", "bspw", "ggf", "evtl", "inkl", "mio", "mrd", "vgl":
		return true
	}
	return false
}

// isHardTerminator ends a sentence anywhere: ellipsis, CJK stop, fullwidth ! and ?, Arabic stop and question mark,
// danda, double danda, Greek question mark.
func isHardTerminator(r rune) bool {
	switch r {
	case 0x2026, 0x3002, 0xFF01, 0xFF1F, 0x06D4, 0x061F, 0x0964, 0x0965, 0x037E:
		return true
	}
	return false
}

// opensNextSentence says that the quote at j, in the run after a hard terminator, belongs to the next sentence. U+201C,
// U+2018 and U+00AB close a German quote but open an English, Chinese or French one, and Chinese puts no space after its
// stop. So a letter or digit directly after the quote means it opens. The ASCII rule needs no check, because a closer run
// followed by a letter is no sentence end there.
func opensNextSentence(rs []rune, j int) bool {
	switch rs[j] {
	case 0x201C, 0x2018, 0x00AB:
		return j+1 < len(rs) && (unicode.IsLetter(rs[j+1]) || unicode.IsDigit(rs[j+1]))
	}
	return false
}

// isSentenceCloser lists the quotes and brackets that stay on the sentence. U+201C, U+2018 and U+00AB are German closers,
// see opensNextSentence for their other use.
func isSentenceCloser(r rune) bool {
	switch r {
	case 0x22, 0x27, 0x201D, 0x2019, 0x201C, 0x2018, 0x00BB, 0x00AB, 0x203A, 0x29, 0x5D, 0x7D, 0xFF09, 0x300D, 0x300F, 0x3011, 0x3009, 0x300B:
		return true
	}
	return false
}

// isSplitComma lists the comma, Arabic comma, ideographic comma, fullwidth comma, semicolon and fullwidth semicolon.
func isSplitComma(r rune) bool {
	switch r {
	case 0x2C, 0x060C, 0x3001, 0xFF0C, 0x3B, 0xFF1B:
		return true
	}
	return false
}

// splitLongSentence cuts a span over limit runes: at the comma nearest its middle, else at the last whitespace before
// the limit, else at the limit.
func splitLongSentence(rs []rune, sp pieceSpan, limit int) []pieceSpan {
	if sp.hi-sp.lo <= limit {
		return []pieceSpan{sp}
	}
	cut := commaCut(rs, sp)
	if cut < 0 {
		cut = whitespaceCut(rs, sp, limit)
	}
	if cut < 0 {
		cut = sp.lo + limit
	}
	left := splitLongSentence(rs, spanOf(rs, sp.start, cut), limit)
	return append(left, splitLongSentence(rs, spanOf(rs, cut, sp.end), limit)...)
}

// commaCut returns the start of the right part for the best comma, or -1. The comma and its whitespace stay on the left.
func commaCut(rs []rune, sp pieceSpan) int {
	mid := (sp.lo + sp.hi) / 2
	best, bestDist := -1, 0
	for i := sp.lo; i < sp.hi; i++ {
		if !isSplitComma(rs[i]) {
			continue
		}
		cut := i + 1
		for cut < sp.hi && IsJSSpace(rs[cut]) {
			cut++
		}
		if i+1-sp.lo < minSplitRunes || sp.hi-cut < minSplitRunes {
			continue
		}
		dist := i - mid
		if dist < 0 {
			dist = -dist
		}
		if best < 0 || dist < bestDist {
			best, bestDist = cut, dist
		}
	}
	return best
}

// whitespaceCut returns the start of the right part after the last whitespace at or before the limit, or -1.
func whitespaceCut(rs []rune, sp pieceSpan, limit int) int {
	for i := sp.lo + limit; i > sp.lo; i-- {
		if IsJSSpace(rs[i]) {
			cut := i + 1
			for cut < sp.hi && IsJSSpace(rs[cut]) {
				cut++
			}
			return cut
		}
	}
	return -1
}

// mergeFragments merges a fragment into the next part, or into the previous one if it is last, when the merged span
// stays within the limit. A merged part is tested again and keeps the join of its last member.
func mergeFragments(rs []rune, parts []pieceSpan, limit int) []pieceSpan {
	isFragment := func(p pieceSpan) bool {
		text := string(rs[p.lo:p.hi])
		return TextWidth(text) < fragmentWidth || WordCount(text) <= fragmentWords
	}
	merged := func(a, b pieceSpan) pieceSpan {
		return pieceSpan{start: a.start, lo: a.lo, hi: b.hi, end: b.end, join: b.join}
	}
	for i := 0; i < len(parts); {
		switch {
		case !isFragment(parts[i]):
			i++
		case i+1 < len(parts):
			if parts[i+1].hi-parts[i].lo <= limit {
				parts[i] = merged(parts[i], parts[i+1])
				parts = append(parts[:i+1], parts[i+2:]...)
			} else {
				i++
			}
		case i > 0 && parts[i].hi-parts[i-1].lo <= limit:
			parts[i-1] = merged(parts[i-1], parts[i])
			parts = parts[:i]
			i--
		default:
			i++
		}
	}
	return parts
}

// UTF16Len counts the UTF-16 units of s, the unit JS string offsets use.
```

```go
package storymaker

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/binary"
	"errors"
	"math"
	"math/rand"
	"os"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

// audioTestFormat describes a WAV file for audioTestWAV.
type audioTestFormat struct {
	tag, channels, bits uint16
	rate                uint32
	align               uint16  // 0 means channels * bits / 8
	sub                 uint16  // first two bytes of the sub-format GUID when tag is 0xFFFE
	dataField           *uint32 // nil means the real data length
	listChunk           bool    // an odd-sized LIST chunk ahead of the data
}

func audioTestWAV(f audioTestFormat, data []byte) []byte {
	le := binary.LittleEndian
	align := f.align
	if align == 0 {
		align = f.channels * f.bits / 8
	}
	var format bytes.Buffer
	binary.Write(&format, le, f.tag)
	binary.Write(&format, le, f.channels)
	binary.Write(&format, le, f.rate)
	binary.Write(&format, le, f.rate*uint32(align))
	binary.Write(&format, le, align)
	binary.Write(&format, le, f.bits)
	if f.tag == 0xFFFE {
		binary.Write(&format, le, uint16(22)) // extension size
		binary.Write(&format, le, f.bits)     // valid bits
		binary.Write(&format, le, uint32(0))  // channel mask
		binary.Write(&format, le, f.sub)      // start of the sub-format GUID
		format.Write(make([]byte, 14))        // rest of the GUID
	}
	var out bytes.Buffer
	out.WriteString("RIFF")
	binary.Write(&out, le, uint32(0)) // the decoder never reads the RIFF size
	out.WriteString("WAVEfmt ")
	binary.Write(&out, le, uint32(format.Len()))
	out.Write(format.Bytes())
	if f.listChunk {
		out.WriteString("LIST")
		binary.Write(&out, le, uint32(3))
		out.Write([]byte{1, 2, 3, 0}) // 3 bytes plus the pad byte
	}
	out.WriteString("data")
	field := uint32(len(data))
	if f.dataField != nil {
		field = *f.dataField
	}
	binary.Write(&out, le, field)
	out.Write(data)
	return out.Bytes()
}

func audioTestS16(values ...int16) []byte {
	var b bytes.Buffer
	binary.Write(&b, binary.LittleEndian, values)
	return b.Bytes()
}

func audioTestS24(values ...int32) []byte {
	var b bytes.Buffer
	for _, v := range values {
		b.Write([]byte{byte(v), byte(v >> 8), byte(v >> 16)})
	}
	return b.Bytes()
}

func audioTestF32(values ...float32) []byte {
	var b bytes.Buffer
	binary.Write(&b, binary.LittleEndian, values)
	return b.Bytes()
}

func audioTestOneMP3(t *testing.T) []byte {
	t.Helper()
	data, err := os.ReadFile("testdata/narration/one.mp3")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return data
}

func TestDecodeAudioWAVFormats(t *testing.T) {
	// Rate 4000: two frames last 0.5 ms, which rounds to 1 ms, the shortest audio the decoder keeps.
	s16 := audioTestFormat{tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 4000}
	withField := func(f audioTestFormat, v uint32) audioTestFormat {
		f.dataField = &v
		return f
	}
	withList := s16
	withList.listChunk = true
	half := audioTestS16(16384, 16384) // 16384 / 32768 = 0.5
	cases := []struct {
		name     string
		format   audioTestFormat
		data     []byte
		channels int
		want     []float32
	}{
		{"s16", s16, audioTestS16(0, 16384, -32768), 1, []float32{0, 0.5, -1}},
		{"s24", audioTestFormat{tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 24, rate: 4000}, audioTestS24(0x400000, -0x800000), 1, []float32{0.5, -1}},
		{"f32 clamps and drops NaN", audioTestFormat{tag: 3, channels: 1, bits: 32, rate: 4000}, audioTestF32(0.25, -0.5, 2, float32(math.NaN())), 1, []float32{0.25, -0.5, 1, 0}},
		{"stereo", audioTestFormat{tag: 1, channels: 2, bits: 16, rate: 4000}, audioTestS16(16384, -16384, 16384, -16384), 2, []float32{0.5, -0.5, 0.5, -0.5}},
		{"extensible s16", audioTestFormat{tag: 0xFFFE, sub: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 4000}, half, 1, []float32{0.5, 0.5}},
		{"extensible f32", audioTestFormat{tag: 0xFFFE, sub: 3, channels: 1, bits: 32, rate: 4000}, audioTestF32(0.25, 0.25), 1, []float32{0.25, 0.25}},
		{"data size 0", withField(s16, 0), half, 1, []float32{0.5, 0.5}},
		{"data size 0xFFFFFFFF", withField(s16, 0xFFFFFFFF), half, 1, []float32{0.5, 0.5}},
		{"data size above the file", withField(s16, 1000), half, 1, []float32{0.5, 0.5}},
		{"a chunk ahead of the data", withList, half, 1, []float32{0.5, 0.5}},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		a, ext, err := DecodeAudio(audioTestWAV(c.format, c.data))
		if err != nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: %v", c.name, err)
			continue
		}
		if ext != "wav" || a.Rate != 4000 || a.Channels != c.channels || len(a.Samples) != len(c.want) {
			t.Errorf("%s: ext %q rate %d channels %d samples %v", c.name, ext, a.Rate, a.Channels, a.Samples)
			continue
		}
		for i, want := range c.want {
			if math.Abs(float64(a.Samples[i]-want)) > 1e-6 {
				t.Errorf("%s: sample %d is %v, want %v", c.name, i, a.Samples[i], want)
			}
		}
	}
}

func TestDecodeAudioWAVRefusals(t *testing.T) {
	for name, format := range map[string]audioTestFormat{
		"rate 3000":   {tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 3000},
		"3 channels":  {tag: 1, channels: 3, bits: 16, rate: 16000},
		"wrong align": {tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 16000, align: 4},
	} {
		if _, _, err := DecodeAudio(audioTestWAV(format, make([]byte, 1200))); err == nil {
			t.Errorf("%s was accepted", name)
		} else if errors.Is(err, ErrUnknownAudio) {
			t.Errorf("%s: the format is known, got %v", name, err)
		}
	}
	empty := audioTestWAV(audioTestFormat{tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 16000}, nil)
	if _, _, err := DecodeAudio(empty); err == nil {
		t.Error("a WAV without samples was accepted")
	}
}

func TestDecodeAudioCapsTheDuration(t *testing.T) {
	// 4000 Hz mono s16 is 8000 bytes per second.
	format := audioTestFormat{tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 4000}
	if _, _, err := DecodeAudio(audioTestWAV(format, make([]byte, 8000*maxDecodedSeconds))); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("600 s: %v", err)
	}
	if _, _, err := DecodeAudio(audioTestWAV(format, make([]byte, 8000*(maxDecodedSeconds+1)))); err == nil {
		t.Error("601 s of WAV was accepted")
	}
	// one.mp3 lasts about 1 s, so a cap of 0 s trips and a cap of 2 s does not. DecodeAudio passes maxDecodedSeconds.
	one := audioTestOneMP3(t)
	if _, err := decodeMP3(one, 0); err == nil {
		t.Error("an MP3 above the cap was accepted")
	}
	if _, err := decodeMP3(one, 2); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("an MP3 under the cap: %v", err)
	}
	if maxDecodedSeconds != 600 {
		t.Errorf("maxDecodedSeconds is %d", maxDecodedSeconds)
	}
}

func TestDecodeAudioMP3(t *testing.T) {
	a, ext, err := DecodeAudio(audioTestOneMP3(t))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if ext != "mp3" || a.Rate != 24000 || a.Channels != 2 || len(a.Samples)%2 != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("ext %q rate %d channels %d samples %d", ext, a.Rate, a.Channels, len(a.Samples))
	}
	// The source lasts 1000 ms. Encoder delay, the Xing frame and padding add under 200 ms.
	// A 24 kHz frame is 24 ms, so one frame below the source length is the lowest allowed value.
	ms := len(a.Samples) / 2 * 1000 / a.Rate
	if ms < 976 || ms > 1200 {
		t.Errorf("decoded %d ms", ms)
	}
	// FFmpeg's sine source has amplitude 1/8. The lossy peak stays well above 0.05.
	peak := 0.0
	for _, s := range a.Samples {
		peak = math.Max(peak, math.Abs(float64(s)))
	}
	if peak < 0.05 || peak > 1 {
		t.Errorf("peak %v", peak)
	}
}

func TestDecodeAudioGarbageMP3IsAnError(t *testing.T) {
	// A valid MPEG-1 Layer III header (128 kbit/s, 44.1 kHz, frame size 417 bytes) with only 64 bytes behind it.
	truncated := append([]byte{0xFF, 0xFB, 0x90, 0x00}, make([]byte, 64)...)
	// An ID3v2 header that claims a tag of about 256 MiB. go-mp3 would allocate it.
	hugeTag := []byte("ID3\x04\x00\x00\x7f\x7f\x7f\x7f")
	for name, data := range map[string][]byte{"truncated": truncated, "huge tag": hugeTag} {
		if _, _, err := DecodeAudio(data); err == nil {
			t.Errorf("%s was accepted", name)
		}
	}
	// Valid headers around noise may decode to noise or fail, but they must return.
	rng := rand.New(rand.NewSource(1))
	var noise bytes.Buffer
	for i := 0; i < 200; i++ {
		frame := make([]byte, 417)
		rng.Read(frame)
		copy(frame, []byte{0xFF, 0xFB, 0x90, 0x00})
		noise.Write(frame)
	}
	_, _, _ = DecodeAudio(noise.Bytes())
}

func TestRecoverMP3TurnsAPanicIntoAnError(t *testing.T) {
	boom := func() (err error) {
		defer recoverMP3(&err)
		panic("bad frame")
	}
	if err := boom(); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "bad frame") {
		t.Fatalf("got %v", err)
	}
}

func TestSniffAudioReadsTheBytes(t *testing.T) {
	wav := audioTestWAV(audioTestFormat{tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 4000}, audioTestS16(1, 2, 3))
	for name, c := range map[string]struct {
		head []byte
		want string
	}{
		"wav":      {wav[:12], "wav"},
		"id3":      {[]byte("ID3"), "mp3"},
		"frame":    {[]byte{0xFF, 0xFB}, "mp3"},
		"adts aac": {[]byte{0xFF, 0xF1}, ""}, // layer bits 00 are reserved in MPEG audio
		"ogg":      {[]byte("OggS"), ""},
		"empty":    {nil, ""},
	} {
		if got := SniffAudio(c.head); got != c.want {
			t.Errorf("%s: got %q, want %q", name, got, c.want)
		}
	}
	if _, ext, err := DecodeAudio(wav); err != nil || ext != "wav" {
		t.Errorf("wav bytes: ext %q, err %v", ext, err)
	}
	if _, ext, err := DecodeAudio([]byte("OggS and more bytes")); !errors.Is(err, ErrUnknownAudio) || ext != "" {
		t.Errorf("ogg bytes: ext %q, err %v", ext, err)
	}
}

type audioTestCountingReader struct {
	*bytes.Reader
	read int64
}

func (c *audioTestCountingReader) Read(p []byte) (int, error) {
	n, err := c.Reader.Read(p)
	c.read += int64(n)
	return n, err
}

func TestProbeAudio(t *testing.T) {
	// 48 kHz mono s16 is 96000 bytes per second, so 5760000 bytes last 60 s.
	file := audioTestWAV(audioTestFormat{tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 48000}, make([]byte, 5760000))
	counting := &audioTestCountingReader{Reader: bytes.NewReader(file)}
	ext, ms, err := ProbeAudio(counting)
	if err != nil || ext != "wav" || ms != 60000 {
		t.Errorf("wav: ext %q, %d ms, err %v", ext, ms, err)
	}
	if counting.read > 4096 {
		t.Errorf("the probe read %d bytes, so it read samples", counting.read)
	}

	// A streamed size takes its length from the file. Music runs up to 3600 s, so the 600 s decode cap does not apply.
	// 4000 Hz mono s16 is 8000 bytes per second, so 601 s is 4808000 bytes.
	zero := uint32(0)
	long := audioTestWAV(audioTestFormat{tag: 1, channels: 1, bits: 16, rate: 4000, dataField: &zero}, make([]byte, 4808000))
	if ext, ms, err = ProbeAudio(bytes.NewReader(long)); err != nil || ext != "wav" || ms != 601000 {
		t.Errorf("long wav: ext %q, %d ms, err %v", ext, ms, err)
	}

	// The same bounds as TestDecodeAudioMP3.
	if ext, ms, err = ProbeAudio(bytes.NewReader(audioTestOneMP3(t))); err != nil || ext != "mp3" || ms < 976 || ms > 1200 {
		t.Errorf("mp3: ext %q, %d ms, err %v", ext, ms, err)
	}

	if _, _, err = ProbeAudio(bytes.NewReader([]byte("OggS"))); !errors.Is(err, ErrUnknownAudio) {
		t.Errorf("ogg: %v", err)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/storymaker/audio_decode.go`:

```go
package storymaker

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/binary"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"math"

	mp3 "github.com/hajimehoshi/go-mp3"
)

// ErrUnknownAudio means the bytes are neither WAV nor MP3.
var ErrUnknownAudio = errors.New("storymaker: unknown audio format")

const maxDecodedSeconds = 600

const (
	minWAVRate        = 4000
	maxWAVRate        = 192000
	maxWAVChunks      = 128
	maxWAVFormatBytes = 4096
	wavTagPCM         = 1
	wavTagFloat       = 3
	wavTagExtensible  = 0xFFFE
	mp3FrameBytes     = 4 // go-mp3 always gives stereo s16
)

var errNoAudio = errors.New("storymaker: the file holds no audio")

// DecodedAudio is decoded PCM.
type DecodedAudio struct {
	Rate, Channels int
	Samples        []float32 // interleaved, -1..1
}

// SniffAudio detects the format from the first bytes: "wav", "mp3" or "".
func SniffAudio(head []byte) string {
	if len(head) >= 12 && string(head[:4]) == "RIFF" && string(head[8:12]) == "WAVE" {
		return "wav"
	}
	if len(head) >= 3 && string(head[:3]) == "ID3" {
		return "mp3"
	}
	// A frame sync, without the reserved MPEG version (01) and the reserved layer (00). The second excludes ADTS AAC.
	if len(head) >= 2 && head[0] == 0xFF && head[1]&0xE0 == 0xE0 && head[1]&0x18 != 0x08 && head[1]&0x06 != 0 {
		return "mp3"
	}
	return ""
}

// DecodeAudio detects the format from the bytes, never from a claimed extension. ext is "wav" or "mp3".
func DecodeAudio(data []byte) (DecodedAudio, string, error) {
	switch SniffAudio(data) {
	case "wav":
		a, err := decodeWAV(data)
		if err != nil {
			return DecodedAudio{}, "", err
		}
		return a, "wav", nil
	case "mp3":
		a, err := decodeMP3(data, maxDecodedSeconds)
		if err != nil {
			return DecodedAudio{}, "", err
		}
		return a, "mp3", nil
	}
	return DecodedAudio{}, "", ErrUnknownAudio
}

// ProbeAudio reads the format and the duration without keeping any samples: the WAV header, or go-mp3's Length.
// Music tracks use it, so the 600 s decode cap does not apply.
func ProbeAudio(r io.ReadSeeker) (ext string, durationMS int, err error) {
	size, err := r.Seek(0, io.SeekEnd)
	if err != nil {
		return "", 0, err
	}
	if _, err := r.Seek(0, io.SeekStart); err != nil {
		return "", 0, err
	}
	head := make([]byte, 12)
	n, err := io.ReadFull(r, head)
	if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, io.EOF) && !errors.Is(err, io.ErrUnexpectedEOF) {
		return "", 0, err
	}
	head = head[:n]
	switch SniffAudio(head) {
	case "wav":
		ms, err := probeWAV(r, size)
		if err != nil {
			return "", 0, err
		}
		return "wav", ms, nil
	case "mp3":
		ms, err := probeMP3(r, head, size)
		if err != nil {
			return "", 0, err
		}
		return "mp3", ms, nil
	}
	return "", 0, ErrUnknownAudio
}

// roundedMS is round(frames*1000/rate).
func roundedMS(frames int64, rate int) int {
	return int((frames*2000 + int64(rate)) / (2 * int64(rate)))
}

type wavHeader struct {
	rate, channels, bits, frameBytes int
	float                            bool
	dataOffset, dataLen              int64
}

// readWAVHeader walks the chunks after the 12-byte RIFF header. A data length of 0, 0xFFFFFFFF or above the file means
// the rest of the file.
func readWAVHeader(r io.ReadSeeker, size int64) (wavHeader, error) {
	var h wavHeader
	pos := int64(12)
	haveFormat := false
	for chunk := 0; chunk < maxWAVChunks; chunk++ {
		if _, err := r.Seek(pos, io.SeekStart); err != nil {
			return h, err
		}
		var head [8]byte
		if _, err := io.ReadFull(r, head[:]); err != nil {
			return h, errors.New("storymaker: the WAV has no data chunk")
		}
		length := int64(binary.LittleEndian.Uint32(head[4:]))
		body := pos + 8
		switch string(head[:4]) {
		case "fmt ":
			if err := readWAVFormat(r, length, &h); err != nil {
				return h, err
			}
			haveFormat = true
		case "data":
			if !haveFormat {
				return h, errors.New("storymaker: the WAV has its data ahead of its format")
			}
			rest := size - body
			if length == 0 || length == 0xFFFFFFFF || length > rest {
				length = rest
			}
			h.dataOffset = body
			h.dataLen = length - length%int64(h.frameBytes)
			return h, nil
		}
		pos = body + length + length%2
	}
	return h, errors.New("storymaker: the WAV has too many chunks")
}

func readWAVFormat(r io.Reader, length int64, h *wavHeader) error {
	if length < 16 || length > maxWAVFormatBytes {
		return fmt.Errorf("storymaker: the WAV format chunk has %d bytes", length)
	}
	b := make([]byte, length)
	if _, err := io.ReadFull(r, b); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("storymaker: the WAV format chunk is cut off: %w", err)
	}
	tag := binary.LittleEndian.Uint16(b)
	h.channels = int(binary.LittleEndian.Uint16(b[2:]))
	h.rate = int(binary.LittleEndian.Uint32(b[4:]))
	align := int(binary.LittleEndian.Uint16(b[12:]))
	h.bits = int(binary.LittleEndian.Uint16(b[14:]))
	h.float = false
	if tag == wavTagExtensible {
		if length < 26 {
			return errors.New("storymaker: the WAV extensible format chunk is too short")
		}
		tag = binary.LittleEndian.Uint16(b[24:])
	}
	switch {
	case tag == wavTagPCM && (h.bits == 16 || h.bits == 24):
	case tag == wavTagFloat && h.bits == 32:
		h.float = true
	default:
		return fmt.Errorf("storymaker: WAV format %#x with %d bits is not supported", tag, h.bits)
	}
	if h.rate < minWAVRate || h.rate > maxWAVRate {
		return fmt.Errorf("storymaker: the WAV rate %d Hz is outside %d..%d", h.rate, minWAVRate, maxWAVRate)
	}
	if h.channels < 1 || h.channels > 2 {
		return fmt.Errorf("storymaker: the WAV has %d channels", h.channels)
	}
	h.frameBytes = h.channels * h.bits / 8
	if align != h.frameBytes {
		return fmt.Errorf("storymaker: the WAV block align is %d, want %d", align, h.frameBytes)
	}
	return nil
}

func decodeWAV(data []byte) (DecodedAudio, error) {
	h, err := readWAVHeader(bytes.NewReader(data), int64(len(data)))
	if err != nil {
		return DecodedAudio{}, err
	}
	frames := h.dataLen / int64(h.frameBytes)
	if frames > int64(maxDecodedSeconds)*int64(h.rate) {
		return DecodedAudio{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: the WAV lasts more than %d s", maxDecodedSeconds)
	}
	if roundedMS(frames, h.rate) == 0 {
		return DecodedAudio{}, errNoAudio
	}
	raw := data[h.dataOffset : h.dataOffset+h.dataLen]
	sampleBytes := h.bits / 8
	samples := make([]float32, len(raw)/sampleBytes)
	for i := range samples {
		b := raw[i*sampleBytes:]
		switch {
		case h.float:
			samples[i] = cleanSample(math.Float32frombits(binary.LittleEndian.Uint32(b)))
		case h.bits == 24:
			v := int32(uint32(b[0])<<8|uint32(b[1])<<16|uint32(b[2])<<24) >> 8
			samples[i] = float32(v) / 8388608
		default:
			samples[i] = float32(int16(binary.LittleEndian.Uint16(b))) / 32768
		}
	}
	return DecodedAudio{Rate: h.rate, Channels: h.channels, Samples: samples}, nil
}

// cleanSample keeps a float sample inside -1..1 and turns NaN into silence.
func cleanSample(v float32) float32 {
	switch {
	case math.IsNaN(float64(v)):
		return 0
	case v > 1:
		return 1
	case v < -1:
		return -1
	}
	return v
}

func probeWAV(r io.ReadSeeker, size int64) (int, error) {
	h, err := readWAVHeader(r, size)
	if err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	ms := roundedMS(h.dataLen/int64(h.frameBytes), h.rate)
	if ms == 0 {
		return 0, errNoAudio
	}
	return ms, nil
}

// recoverMP3 turns a go-mp3 panic into an error. Call it directly with defer.
func recoverMP3(err *error) {
	if r := recover(); r != nil {
		*err = fmt.Errorf("storymaker: the MP3 decoder failed: %v", r)
	}
}

// checkID3Size refuses an ID3v2 tag that claims more bytes than the file holds, because go-mp3 allocates the claimed
// size. The size formula is go-mp3's own (source.go skipTags).
func checkID3Size(head []byte, total int64) error {
	if len(head) < 3 || string(head[:3]) != "ID3" {
		return nil
	}
	if len(head) < 10 {
		return errors.New("storymaker: the MP3 tag header is cut off")
	}
	size := int64(head[6])<<21 | int64(head[7])<<14 | int64(head[8])<<7 | int64(head[9])
	if size > total-10 {
		return errors.New("storymaker: the MP3 tag is larger than the file")
	}
	return nil
}

// decodeMP3 reads in chunks and stops with an error above maxSeconds, so the cap holds before the allocation.
func decodeMP3(data []byte, maxSeconds int) (a DecodedAudio, err error) {
	defer recoverMP3(&err)
	if err := checkID3Size(data, int64(len(data))); err != nil {
		return DecodedAudio{}, err
	}
	d, err := mp3.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(data))
	if err != nil {
		return DecodedAudio{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: decode MP3: %w", err)
	}
	rate := d.SampleRate()
	maxFrames := int64(maxSeconds) * int64(rate)
	tooLong := fmt.Errorf("storymaker: the MP3 lasts more than %d s", maxSeconds)
	if d.Length()/mp3FrameBytes > maxFrames {
		return DecodedAudio{}, tooLong
	}
	samples := make([]float32, 0, d.Length()/2)
	// go-mp3 keeps whole samples in its buffer and this size is a multiple of 4, so n stays aligned.
	buf := make([]byte, 64*1024)
	for {
		n, readErr := d.Read(buf)
		if int64(len(samples)/2)+int64(n/mp3FrameBytes) > maxFrames {
			return DecodedAudio{}, tooLong
		}
		for i := 0; i+1 < n; i += 2 {
			samples = append(samples, float32(int16(binary.LittleEndian.Uint16(buf[i:])))/32768)
		}
		if errors.Is(readErr, io.EOF) {
			break
		}
		if readErr != nil {
			return DecodedAudio{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: decode MP3: %w", readErr)
		}
	}
	if roundedMS(int64(len(samples)/2), rate) == 0 {
		return DecodedAudio{}, errNoAudio
	}
	return DecodedAudio{Rate: rate, Channels: 2, Samples: samples}, nil
}

func probeMP3(r io.ReadSeeker, head []byte, size int64) (ms int, err error) {
	defer recoverMP3(&err)
	if err := checkID3Size(head, size); err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	if _, err := r.Seek(0, io.SeekStart); err != nil {
		return 0, err
	}
	d, err := mp3.NewDecoder(r)
	if err != nil {
		return 0, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: decode MP3: %w", err)
	}
	if d.Length() <= 0 {
		return 0, errNoAudio
	}
	return roundedMS(d.Length()/mp3FrameBytes, d.SampleRate()), nil
}
```

```go
// envelopeOfRuns builds an envelope whose windows inside the runs (ms) hold level 170 (-20 dBFS) and 0 elsewhere.
func envelopeOfRuns(durationMS int, runs ...[2]int) Envelope {
	levels := make(Levels, (durationMS+9)/10)
	for _, r := range runs {
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `internal/storymaker/envelope.go`:

```go
package storymaker

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"math"
	"strconv"
)

const (
	envelopeRate     = 100 // windows per second
	envelopeWindowMS = 10
	speechLevel      = 85  // level byte of -40 dBFS
	gapBridgeMS      = 100 // runs separated by less are bridged
	inMarginMS       = 40
	outMarginMS      = 100
	sentencePauseMS  = 350
	splitPauseMS     = 150
)

// Levels marshals as a JSON array of integers 0..255. A plain []uint8 would become base64.
type Levels []uint8

// MarshalJSON writes an array, also for nil.
func (l Levels) MarshalJSON() ([]byte, error) {
	out := make([]byte, 0, len(l)*4+2)
	out = append(out, '[')
	for i, v := range l {
		if i > 0 {
			out = append(out, ',')
		}
		out = strconv.AppendUint(out, uint64(v), 10)
	}
	return append(out, ']'), nil
}

// UnmarshalJSON reads an array of integers and refuses any value outside 0..255.
func (l *Levels) UnmarshalJSON(b []byte) error {
	var raw []int
	if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &raw); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	out := make(Levels, len(raw))
	for i, v := range raw {
		if v < 0 || v > 255 {
			return fmt.Errorf("storymaker: level %d is outside 0..255", v)
		}
		out[i] = uint8(v)
	}
	*l = out
	return nil
}

// Envelope is the stored 10 ms loudness curve of one audio file.
type Envelope struct {
	V          int    `json:"v"` // 1
	DurationMS int    `json:"duration_ms"`
	Rate       int    `json:"rate"` // 100
	Levels     Levels `json:"levels"`
}

// levelByte is round((clamp(dBFS,-60,0)+60)*255/60), silence 0.
func levelByte(power float64) uint8 {
	if power <= 0 {
		return 0
	}
	db := 10 * math.Log10(power)
	if db < -60 {
		db = -60
	}
	if db > 0 {
		db = 0
	}
	return uint8(math.Round((db + 60) * 255 / 60))
}

// levelPower is the inverse of levelByte: 0 when b is 0, else 10^((b*60/255-60)/10).
func levelPower(b uint8) float64 {
	if b == 0 {
		return 0
	}
	return math.Pow(10, (float64(b)*60/255-60)/10)
}

// ComputeEnvelope measures mean power across channels per window. Window i covers the frames floor(i*rate/100) to
// floor((i+1)*rate/100)-1, and a final partial window counts. A fixed window would drift at 22050 Hz.
func ComputeEnvelope(a DecodedAudio) Envelope {
	env := Envelope{V: 1, Rate: envelopeRate, Levels: Levels{}}
	if a.Rate <= 0 || a.Channels <= 0 || len(a.Samples) < a.Channels {
		return env
	}
	channels := int64(a.Channels)
	frames := int64(len(a.Samples)) / channels
	rate := int64(a.Rate)
	env.DurationMS = roundedMS(frames, a.Rate)
	windows := (frames*envelopeRate + rate - 1) / rate
	env.Levels = make(Levels, windows)
	for i := int64(0); i < windows; i++ {
		first := i * rate / envelopeRate
		end := (i + 1) * rate / envelopeRate
		if end > frames {
			end = frames
		}
		if end <= first {
			continue
		}
		sum := 0.0
		samples := a.Samples[first*channels : end*channels]
		for _, s := range samples {
			sum += float64(s) * float64(s)
		}
		env.Levels[i] = levelByte(sum / float64(len(samples)))
	}
	return env
}

// SpeechRuns lists the runs at or above -40 dBFS, in ms of the file, ascending, with gaps under 100 ms bridged.
func (e Envelope) SpeechRuns() [][2]int {
	var runs [][2]int
	for i := 0; i < len(e.Levels); {
		if e.Levels[i] < speechLevel {
			i++
			continue
		}
		j := i
		for j < len(e.Levels) && e.Levels[j] >= speechLevel {
			j++
		}
		start, end := i*envelopeWindowMS, j*envelopeWindowMS
		if end > e.DurationMS {
			end = e.DurationMS
		}
		i = j
		if start >= end {
			continue
		}
		if n := len(runs); n > 0 && start-runs[n-1][1] < gapBridgeMS {
			runs[n-1][1] = end
		} else {
			runs = append(runs, [2]int{start, end})
		}
	}
	return runs
}

// ParseEnvelope reads a stored env.json. These are ordinary workspace files, so nothing in them is trusted:
// v == 1, rate == 100, 0 < duration_ms <= 600000, and len(levels) equals ceil(duration_ms/10) within 1.
func ParseEnvelope(data []byte) (Envelope, error) {
	var e Envelope
	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &e); err != nil {
		return Envelope{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: envelope: %w", err)
	}
	if e.V != 1 {
		return Envelope{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: envelope version %d", e.V)
	}
	if e.Rate != envelopeRate {
		return Envelope{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: envelope rate %d", e.Rate)
	}
	if e.DurationMS <= 0 || e.DurationMS > maxDecodedSeconds*1000 {
		return Envelope{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: envelope duration %d ms", e.DurationMS)
	}
	want := (e.DurationMS + envelopeWindowMS - 1) / envelopeWindowMS
	if n := len(e.Levels); n < want-1 || n > want+1 {
		return Envelope{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: envelope has %d levels, want %d", n, want)
	}
	return e, nil
}

// AudioPieceInput is one stored piece of a block.
type AudioPieceInput struct {
	Hash, Ext string
	Chars     int
	Join      string
	Env       Envelope
}

// AudioPiece says where a piece plays inside its block and which range of its file is used.
type AudioPiece struct {
	Hash  string `json:"hash"`
	Ext   string `json:"ext"`
	AtMS  int    `json:"at_ms"`
	InMS  int    `json:"in_ms"`
	OutMS int    `json:"out_ms"`
	Chars int    `json:"chars"`
}

// AudioBlock is a measured block as the /narration answer, Plan.AudioInfo and the mix see it.
type AudioBlock struct {
	ID         string       `json:"id"`
	Kind       string       `json:"kind"`
	Beat       *int         `json:"beat,omitempty"`
	Speaker    string       `json:"speaker"`
	DurationMS int          `json:"duration_ms"`
	Spans      [][2]int     `json:"spans"`
	Levels     Levels       `json:"levels"`
	Pieces     []AudioPiece `json:"pieces"`
	SpeechOK   bool         `json:"speech_ok"`
}

func joinPauseMS(join string) int {
	switch join {
	case JoinSentence:
		return sentencePauseMS
	case JoinSplit:
		return splitPauseMS
	}
	return 0
}

// AssembleBlock is the one function behind /narration, Plan.AudioInfo and the mix. Each piece plays only its used range
// and an explicit pause separates pieces. A piece with no speech run plays whole and clears SpeechOK.
func AssembleBlock(b NarrationBlock, pieces []AudioPieceInput, fps int) AudioBlock {
	out := AudioBlock{
		ID: b.ID, Kind: b.Kind, Speaker: b.Speaker,
		Spans: [][2]int{}, Levels: Levels{}, Pieces: make([]AudioPiece, 0, len(pieces)), SpeechOK: true,
	}
	if b.Kind == BlockKindDialogue {
		beat := b.Beat
		out.Beat = &beat
	}
	at, pause := 0, 0
	for _, p := range pieces {
		at += pause
		in, end := 0, p.Env.DurationMS
		runs := p.Env.SpeechRuns()
		if len(runs) == 0 {
			out.SpeechOK = false
		} else {
			in = max(0, runs[0][0]-inMarginMS)
			end = min(p.Env.DurationMS, runs[len(runs)-1][1]+outMarginMS)
		}
		out.Pieces = append(out.Pieces, AudioPiece{Hash: p.Hash, Ext: p.Ext, AtMS: at, InMS: in, OutMS: end, Chars: p.Chars})
		for _, r := range runs {
			first, last := max(r[0], in), min(r[1], end)
			if first < last {
				out.Spans = append(out.Spans, [2]int{at + first - in, at + last - in})
			}
		}
		at += end - in
		pause = joinPauseMS(p.Join)
	}
	out.DurationMS = at
	out.Levels = blockLevels(out, pieces, fps)
	return out
}

// blockLevels gives one level per frame. The block is a 10 ms grid: a bin holds the piece's level byte at the matching
// file position, or 0 in a pause. Frame k averages power over bins k*100/fps to (k+1)*100/fps-1.
func blockLevels(block AudioBlock, pieces []AudioPieceInput, fps int) Levels {
	if fps <= 0 || block.DurationMS <= 0 || len(block.Pieces) == 0 {
		return Levels{}
	}
	frames := (block.DurationMS*fps + 999) / 1000
	bins := (block.DurationMS + envelopeWindowMS - 1) / envelopeWindowMS
	out := make(Levels, frames)
	piece := 0
	for k := 0; k < frames; k++ {
		lo := k * 100 / fps
		hi := (k+1)*100/fps - 1
		if hi < lo {
			hi = lo
		}
		if hi > bins-1 {
			hi = bins - 1
		}
		sum := 0.0
		for j := lo; j <= hi; j++ {
			t := j * envelopeWindowMS
			for piece+1 < len(block.Pieces) && t >= block.Pieces[piece+1].AtMS {
				piece++
			}
			sum += levelPower(binLevel(block.Pieces[piece], pieces[piece].Env.Levels, t))
		}
		if hi >= lo {
			out[k] = levelByte(sum / float64(hi-lo+1))
		}
	}
	return out
}

// binLevel reads the level at block time t, or 0 outside the used range of the piece.
func binLevel(p AudioPiece, levels Levels, t int) uint8 {
	if t < p.AtMS || t >= p.AtMS+p.OutMS-p.InMS {
		return 0
	}
	i := (p.InMS + t - p.AtMS) / envelopeWindowMS
	if i >= len(levels) {
		return 0
	}
	return levels[i]
}
```

```go
// audioStoreStory has one narration block of two sentences. Each is at least 20 wide and has more
// than 3 words, so the merge rule of SplitPieces leaves two pieces.
func audioStoreStory() *Story {
	return &Story{
		Schema: Schema, Title: "Audio",
		Format: Format{Aspect: "16:9", FPS: 30},
		Style:  Style{Palette: DefaultPalette, Font: DefaultFont, Captions: true},
		Voice:  &Voice{Provider: "sanotts", Language: "de"},
		Cast:   []CastMember{},
		Scenes: []Scene{{ID: "s1", Narration: "Der erste Satz ist lang genug. Der zweite Satz ist auch lang genug."}},
	}
}

// audioStoreEnvelope passes ParseEnvelope: v 1, rate 100 and ceil(1000/10) = 100 levels.
func audioStoreEnvelope() Envelope {
	levels := make(Levels, 100)
	for i := range levels {
		levels[i] = 170 // -20 dBFS: round((-20+60)*255/60) = 170, above the speech threshold of byte 85
	}
	return Envelope{V: 1, DurationMS: 1000, Rate: 100, Levels: levels}
}

	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return ps, mem, proj.ID
}

func TestAudioAnswerNeedsAudioAndAValidEnvelopeForEveryPiece(t *testing.T) {
	ps, mem, id := audioStoreProject(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	story := audioStoreStory()
	block := Blocks(story)[0]
	pieces := PiecesOf(block)
	if len(pieces) != 2 {
		t.Fatalf("pieces: %+v", pieces)
	}
	first, second := PieceKey(block.Voice, pieces[0].Text), PieceKey(block.Voice, pieces[1].Text)
	firstChars, secondChars := runeLen(pieces[0].Text), runeLen(pieces[1].Text)
	dir := testRoot + "/" + id + "/audio/narration/"
	answer := func() (AudioAnswer, string) {
		t.Helper()
		ans, err := ps.AudioAnswer(ctx, id, story, nil)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		raw, err := json.Marshal(ans)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		return ans, string(raw)
	}
	wantMissing := func(chars int) {
		t.Helper()
		ans, raw := answer()
		if want := []MissingBlock{{SceneID: "s1", BlockID: "s1:n", Chars: chars}}; !reflect.DeepEqual(ans.Missing, want) {
			t.Fatalf("missing = %+v, want %+v", ans.Missing, want)
		}
		if ans.Chars != (CharCounts{Total: firstChars + secondChars, Missing: chars}) {
			t.Fatalf("chars = %+v", ans.Chars)
		}
		if !strings.Contains(raw, `"narration":{}`) || !strings.Contains(raw, `"music":null`) {
			t.Fatalf("answer: %s", raw)
		}
	}

	wantMissing(firstChars + secondChars)

	// Audio without an envelope counts as missing.
	if _, err := mem.WriteFile(ctx, dir+first+".wav", []byte("stored audio"), ""); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	wantMissing(firstChars + secondChars)

	// WriteEnvelope fills it without touching the audio, so no provider call is needed.
	if err := ps.WriteEnvelope(ctx, id, first, audioStoreEnvelope()); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	wantMissing(secondChars)
	if data, _, err := mem.ReadFile(ctx, dir+first+".wav"); err != nil || string(data) != "stored audio" {
		t.Fatalf("audio after WriteEnvelope: %q, %v", data, err)
	}

	// An invalid env.json counts as missing too: 2000000000 ms is over the 600000 ms limit of ParseEnvelope.
	if err := ps.WritePiece(ctx, id, second, "mp3", []byte("second audio"), audioStoreEnvelope()); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if _, err := mem.WriteFile(ctx, dir+second+".env.json", []byte(`{"v":1,"duration_ms":2000000000,"rate":100,"levels":[]}`), ""); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	wantMissing(secondChars)

	// With both envelopes valid the block is complete and the list stays an empty array.
	if err := ps.WriteEnvelope(ctx, id, second, audioStoreEnvelope()); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	ans, raw := answer()
	if ans.Missing == nil || len(ans.Missing) != 0 || ans.Chars.Missing != 0 || !strings.Contains(raw, `"missing":[]`) {
		t.Fatalf("answer: %s", raw)
	}
	blocks := ans.Narration["s1"].Blocks
	if len(blocks) != 1 || blocks[0].ID != "s1:n" || len(blocks[0].Pieces) != 2 ||
		blocks[0].Pieces[0].Hash != first || blocks[0].Pieces[0].Ext != "wav" ||
		blocks[0].Pieces[1].Hash != second || blocks[0].Pieces[1].Ext != "mp3" {
		t.Fatalf("blocks: %+v", blocks)
	}
	if !reflect.DeepEqual(ans.Info().Narration, ans.Narration) {
		t.Fatalf("info: %+v", ans.Info())
	}
}

	} {

		t.Errorf("another resolver error must be returned: %v", err)

func TestOpenAudioReadsWhatWritePieceKept(t *testing.T) {
	ps, _, id := audioStoreProject(t)
	ctx := context.Background()
	hash := strings.Repeat("a", 64)
	// A second write of an existing piece is not an error and keeps the first audio.
	for _, data := range []string{"first", "second"} {
		if err := ps.WritePiece(ctx, id, hash, "wav", []byte(data), audioStoreEnvelope()); err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
	}
	r, info, err := ps.OpenAudio(ctx, id, "narration", hash+".wav")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	defer r.Close()
	data, err := io.ReadAll(r)
	if err != nil || string(data) != "first" || info.Name != hash+".wav" || info.Size != int64(len("first")) {
		t.Fatalf("read %q, %v, info %+v", data, err, info)
	}
	for _, bad := range [][2]string{{"exports", hash + ".wav"}, {"narration", "../story.json"}, {"narration", strings.Repeat("A", 64) + ".wav"}} {
		if _, err := ps.AudioPath(id, bad[0], bad[1]); !errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidArgument) {
			t.Errorf("AudioPath(%q, %q) = %v", bad[0], bad[1], err)
		}
	}
	if err := ps.RemovePiece(ctx, id, hash, "wav"); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if _, _, err := ps.OpenAudio(ctx, id, "narration", hash+".wav"); !errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
		t.Errorf("OpenAudio after RemovePiece: %v", err)
	}
	files, err := ps.PieceFiles(ctx, id)
	if err != nil || len(files) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("PieceFiles after RemovePiece: %+v, %v", files, err)
	}
}
```

```go
// Open implements Store: a reader over a copy of the bytes. ModTime stays zero, because MemStore
// keeps no times.
func (m *MemStore) Open(_ context.Context, p string) (io.ReadSeekCloser, FileInfo, error) {
	m.mu.Lock()
	defer m.mu.Unlock()
	key := memClean(p)
	data, ok := m.files[key]
	if !ok {
		return nil, FileInfo{}, fmt.Errorf("storymaker: %s: %w", p, fs.ErrNotExist)
	}
	info := FileInfo{Name: path.Base(key), Size: int64(len(data)), MIME: desktop.MIMETypeForName(key)}
	return memFile{bytes.NewReader(append([]byte(nil), data...))}, info, nil
}

// memFile adds the Close that io.ReadSeekCloser needs to a bytes.Reader.
type memFile struct{ *bytes.Reader }

// Close implements io.Closer.
func (memFile) Close() error { return nil }

func (m *MemStore) mkdirs(dir string) {
```

```go
// Open implements storymaker.Store over OpenPreviewFile, which has no size limit, so audio above
// the 50 MB limit of ReadFile can still be served.
func (st *storymakerStore) Open(ctx context.Context, p string) (io.ReadSeekCloser, storymaker.FileInfo, error) {
	svc, err := st.service()
	if err != nil {
		return nil, storymaker.FileInfo{}, err
	}
	file, entry, mimeType, err := svc.OpenPreviewFile(ctx, p)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, storymaker.FileInfo{}, mapStoreError(err)
	}
	return file, storymaker.FileInfo{Name: entry.Name, Size: entry.Size, ModTime: entry.ModTime, MIME: mimeType}, nil
}
```
